Industry View In-Line
CPO/NPO and micro     VCSEL based   optical solutions are the
focus for networking, while HVDC takes center stage for power.
Higher bandwidth and better power efficiency: In the networking space, the
migration into higher bandwidth continues, as players like Accton and Celestica
showcased their 1.6T network switches. We think a bigger theme is the effort to
improve the power efficiency of current mainstream pluggable optical solutions.

First off, one well-discussed approach is the adoption CPO or NPO, and it seems the
latter is gaining more attention due to the technical challenges in the former. Spec
standardization, such as CPX connectors, could further accelerate the proliferation,
in our view. Secondly, copper could still help, as Credo has its 1.6T AEC cables for
inter-tray transmission while many other vendors (Amphenol, TE Connectivity,
Molex, etc.) have their 448G CPC solutions for in-tray transmission. Lastly, the micro

VCSEL based optical modules from Credo and ALAB represent another power
efficient solution (12W/end at 1,6T), which has reliability comparable to copper
(100M hours of MTBF/MTBI) for a transmission distance of 50-100m. We believe
this micro VCSEL based solution could potentially replace the AEC for shorter
distance transmission when copper hits the physical limit in the medium term and
be integrated with the CPO architecture in the longer term. In sum, we believe the

above-mentioned solutions are likely to co-exist for different use cases.
800VDC for power delivery taking the center stage: While the Vera Rubin NVL72
rack already has the in-rack power shelf reference design (110KW/shelf at 3+1
redundancy), Nvidia unveiled another reference design this time, using one 660KW
800VDC power rack to support two Vera Rubin NVL72 racks. Moreover, we also
saw a few ±400VDC reference designs as well. Our conversations with companies

on the exhibition floor indicate that the HVDC adoption should start from late
2026, but likely at a small scale extending into 2027. The next checkpoint will be
Vera Rubin Ultra racks, where increasing power consumption and more hardware
devices for inter-rack scale-up network might take up more pace on the rack, though
the liquid cooled power shelf  should ease the space budget a bit. We believe both
solutions are progressing and will closely monitor the dynamics.

Stock picks: We believe that Bizlink (3665.TW) should be a beneficiary of the
proliferation of the micro VCSEL based optical solution, as it is the assembly partner
for Credo and potentially others as well. Chroma (2360.TW) should see drivers
from power testing, CPU/GPU/TPU SL T/FT, and photonics testing (pluggable/
CPO)tools. Delta( (2308.TW) and Lite-On (2301.TW) should continue to grow with
the increasing power consumption of AI clusters, but more meaningful growth

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

1.6T network switch and 1.6T transceiver/AEC
CPO remains one of the focus in the networking space during the OCP Global Summit.
Edgecore and  Celestica showcased its 1.6T network switches based on Tomahawk 6 ASIC,
both for the regular model and the CPO model. Moreover, Edgecore also had its OCS

On the cable side, Credo showcased its 1.6T AEC, but did not comment on the roadmap of
its 3.2T AEC. Our checks in the exhibition with industry players indicate that the 3.2T AEC
might be a very challenging project, though efforts are still in place.

CPX and NPX all marching forward
Though CPO remains as the holy grail in the space, its yield issue and lower serviceability
still present challenges to the wider adoption in the near term. We believe that the efforts
will continue, but the industry is simultaneously pursuing other more practical solutions

to sort out the power consumption issue of the existing pluggable transceivers,  including
NPO (near-packaged optics), CPC (co-packaged copper) and NPC (near-packaged copper)
Compared to CPO, NPO compromises on the power consumption, but in exchange it eases
the stringent requirement on alignment and serviceability. Given its better technology
readiness and more mature supply chain, we think it might see good traction in the
coming years.

VCSEL based optical solutions gaining more attention
In addition to CPO/NPO/CPC/NPC designs, we see the micro VCSEL based optical
solutions are gaining more attention too. The value proposition is to leverage the wide
and slow architecture for a relatively short transmission distance of 50-100m. It can
achieve 1-2 pJ/bit of power consumption vs. 5-10pJ/bit for CPO and NPO architecture. The

current generation can enable 32Gbps of bandwidth per lane and the 100-200Gbps of
bandwidth per lane is in the roadmap.

12W/end to support 50m of transmission distance with the copper level reliability (100m

(see the report). Longer term, we believe that these initiatives, along with the shuffle box
and FA alignment process, should help its diversify its data interconnect exposure more
into the optical space, vs. current concentration on copper.

In sum, different solutions of data transmission are emerging, trying to improve the power
consumption of the currently mainstream pluggable transceivers. We believe that these
solutions will probably co exist for a while, as different use case could need different
combination of cost and benefits.

Power becoming another bottleneck in the AI deployment
HVDC adoption emerging from Vera Rubin
In the power space, HVDC has been one of the key focuses in the AI cluster, as the rapid
increase in AI rack power density is creating growing challenges for conventional power-
distribution architectures. As rack-level power consumption rising from tens of kilowatts
to several hundreds of KWs, electrical losses, cable size, thermal management
requirements, and overall system complexity increase significantly. Under traditional low-

voltage power architectures, delivering larger amounts of power requires proportionally
higher current, which increases resistive losses and places greater stress on cables,
connectors, busbars, and power-conversion equipment.
At the same time, every power-conversion stage introduces efficiency losses and
generates additional heat. As AI deployments scale into multi-megawatt and eventually
gigawatt-level campuses, even small efficiency improvements can translate into

### Page 10

M
Idea
10
meaningful reductions in operating costs, cooling requirements, and infrastructure
investment. Consequently, the industry is increasingly focused on power architectures
that can reduce conversion stages, lower current levels, and improve overall power-
delivery efficiency.
Against this backdrop, high-voltage direct current (HVDC) is emerging as one of the most
important architectural upgrades for next-generation AI infrastructure. By increasing

distribution voltage, HVDC allows the same amount of power to be delivered at a lower
current, reducing conduction losses and improving system efficiency. Higher-voltage
architectures also improve scalability for ultra-high-density rack deployments by
alleviating some of the physical and thermal constraints associated with conventional
power-distribution systems.
Exhibit 18: Better power efficiency for 800VDC architecture
Source: Delta
Exhibit 19: Better efficiency leads to cost savings

Source: Delta
In a joint keynote speech by Delta and Nvidia, the roadmap illustrates the industry's
gradual migration toward higher-voltage power architectures. Earlier AI server generations
primarily utilized conventional power-delivery schemes centered around rack-level 48V
distribution. As rack power requirements increased with Blackwell-generation systems,
industry participants began evaluating higher-voltage architectures capable of supporting

substantially greater power density. Looking ahead, the industry is increasingly converging
around 800VDC as a potential next-generation standard for large-scale AI deployments,
particularly as rack-level power consumption approaches several hundred kilowatts. The
transition reflects the need to improve power-distribution efficiency while supporting the
continued growth of AI computing infrastructure.
Though we also saw the 800VDC power rack reference design for the Vera Rubin rack in a

1-to-2 configuration (one 660KW power rack supporting two Vera Rubin racks), we think
the adoption of this solution might be relatively low, since the Vera Rubin rack also has a
reference design for the in-rack power shelf design

Morgan Stanley Research 11

Source: Delta
Exhibit 21: 800VDC reference design for VR
Source: Delta
Liquid cooled power shelf on the horizon
The next key check point to watch should be the Vera Rubin Ultra generation, as the
power consumption per rack shall continue to increase and there might be more hardware
devices to enable the inter-rack scale up network, both could take up more space on the
IT rack. That said, the power shelf will shift from air cooling into liquid cooling, which

could narrow the power shelf height by 1U to free up some space as well. Our checks
indicate that both the in-rack power shelf and 800VDC power rack solutions are ongoing
now, and we believe that rack space, power redundancy and system reliability are all the
factors being considered. We'll closely monitor developments on this front.
Exhibit 22: Liquid cooled power shelf
Source: Megmeet
Exhibit 23: Liquid cooled power shelf in rack
Source: Megmeet

Solid state transformers on the Nvidia RVL for the first time
In the MGX ecosystem board, where Nvidia usually list the recommenced vendor lists and
product samples, we saw SST this time. Delta, Heron. DG Matrax, Sungrow, Solaredge,
Megmeet, Luxshare, etc.

Exhibit 24: SST vendors on the Nvidia MGX ecosystem board
Source: Morgan Stanley
Looking further ahead, Solid-State Transformers (SSTs) represent a potential next-
generation evolution of power infrastructure. Unlike traditional transformers that operate
primarily as passive devices, SSTs leverage power electronics to provide voltage
conversion, power conditioning, and bidirectional energy management within a single
platform. This enables greater controllability and flexibility while supporting increasingly

digitalized power networks.
Although SSTs remain at an early stage of commercialization and face challenges related
to economics, reliability, and large-scale deployment, the technology aligns well with long-
term data center trends. As AI campuses evolve into utility-scale power consumers
incorporating energy storage, distributed generation, and advanced power management,
SSTs could eventually become a key enabling technology.

### Page 13

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 13
Therefore, while HVDC is expected to drive the next phase of power architecture
upgrades, SSTs represent a longer-term technology roadmap that could further transform
how future data centers distribute and manage electricity.

According to Infineon, SST could represent an emerging >$1bn market opportunity by
2030, as the technology begins to replace a portion of the >$15bn conventional
transformer market, particularly in smaller-power applications. While SST penetration is
expected to remain relatively modest versus the overall transformer market, the
opportunity is significant for relevant players in the longer term.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Lite-On Technology (2301.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 17x based on average 2027-28 EPS. We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 47% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.4, versus
the stock's 16x P/E and 2% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 7.2.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger optical business growth
n Stronger data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand growth

n Downtrend in raw material prices
Risks to Downside
n Macro demand weakness
n Weaker data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand slowdown
n Uptrend in raw material prices
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.

Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth
n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 45x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of

~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 45x is justified by the 61% operating profit CAGR we project for
2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand

to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

### Page 1

M
Idea
3Q GM likely to decline on higher costs and slower new product adoption…
Lotes reported unaudited 3Q26 revenue of NT$9,924M (+4% q/q, +18% y/y). Higher
copper, gold and plastics costs for legacy products, coupled with slower new
product adoption, should continue to pressure GM. We forecast 3Q GM of 44.6% (-
90bps q/q), below consensus of 45.8% (+30bps q/q). However, a decline in opex to
NT$2.0B, consistent with management commentary, should support operating

profit growth of 17% q/q to NT$2,452M, albeit still 6% below consensus. We
forecast NP of NT$2,174M (+10% q/q) and EPS of NT$19.36, 4% below the Street.
…but we see 3Q as the GM trough, with recovery beginning in 4Q: Following
slower  server platform transitions, we expect adoption to accelerate from 4Q26.
Socket production for AMD's Venice CPU will ramp from 4Q26 and socket
production for Intel’s Oak Stream CPU will ramp in 1Q27. These transitions should

enable Lotes to reprice products and pass through higher input costs, supporting
margin recovery. Our industry checks also point to a rebound in ODM server board
production in 4Q, following a modest 3Q decline. We forecast 4Q revenue of ~NT
$10.5B (+5% q/q), GM of 45.9% (+130bps q/q) and EPS of NT$21.11 (+9% q/q).
2027 - margin recovery and new AI product ramps should drive earnings growth:
Rising adoption of new CPU platforms should support socket margin recovery,

alongside growing contributions from QD, SOCAMM and NPO. QD reached 2–3% of
revenue in September, with further growth expected in 4Q26 and 2027, and we
forecast SOCAMM will reach ~7% of revenue in CY27. Lotes has two NPO
opportunities, one through LINTES and another directly, with the direct program
expected to ramp in 2H27. Although initial contributions should remain modest, we
see NPO as a longer-term AI data center growth opportunity and a potential re-

rating catalyst. Overall, we see the AI revenue mix, including QD, SOCAMM and
NPO, rising from ~5% in CY25 to slightly above 20% by CY28.
Upgrade to OW and raise PT by 15% to NT$2,350, driven by our estimate increases
for CY27-28. Our new PT implies 22x 2027e P/E and 16.5x 2028e P/E, above the past
3-year average of 17.4x, which we view as reasonable amid a rising AI revenue mix.

re-rating.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
3Q26 Results Preview
3Q26 preview:  We forecast revenue of NT$9.92B (+4% q/q, +18% y/y), broadly in line
with the Street. Although gross margin should decline 80bps q/q to 44.6%, a 12%
sequential reduction in operating expenses should lift operating margin 270bps q/q to
24.7%. Nevertheless, this remains 130bps below consensus, implying operating profit of
NT$2.45bn, 6% below the Street. We therefore see potential for an earnings miss, as we

estimate net profit of ~NT$2,174M (+10% q/q, -6% y/y), for EPS of NT$19.36, 4% below
the Street.

AI revenue mix - a sharp step-up in 2027 should strengthen the structural growth
story: We forecast AI revenue contribution will rise from ~5% in CY25  to slightly above

20% by CY28, with the largest increase occurring in 2027. Rising adoption of QD and
SOCAMM connectors should drive the initial acceleration, while NPO sockets provide an
additional growth opportunity, with small-volume contributions expected in 2027 and
larger volumes in 2028. We believe this expanding AI exposure, alongside expected margin
recovery in CPU sockets, should strengthen Lotes’ earnings growth profile and support a
potential valuation re-rating as investors look beyond near-term cost pressures.

### Page 6

6
Price Target Discussion and Valuation Methodology
Our price target increases ~15%, to NT$2,350 from NT$2,045, driven mainly by our
higher earnings estimates for 2027-28.
Our new PT implies ~22x 2027e P/E, slightly above its three-year average forward P/E of
17.4x, but still below that of peer Amphenol (25-35x 2027e P/E, based on Visible Alpha
consensus). We think Lotes stock could  re-rate to a higher valuation multiple if margins
hold up well and Lotes' exposure to AI PCs and AI servers grows in the coming years.

We continue to derive our PT, which is our base case scenario value, from a residual
income model (RIM), consistent with the methodology we apply to the rest of our tech
hardware coverage universe. To calculate the intrinsic value of the stock, we use the
company's beginning equity plus the present value of all expected residual income of
earnings in excess of the cost of capital. The RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of
capital and negative when it is below the cost of capital.

Our key RIM assumptions (all unchanged) include a cost of equity of 9.2% (beta of ~1.0,
equity risk premium of ~8.7% and risk-free rate of ~1.0%), a medium-term growth rate of
12.5% and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Our bull and bear case values rise to NT$3,400 and NT1,285, respectively, in line with
the increase in magnitude of our base case scenario.

▪ We believe GM will trough in 3Q and start
to recover from 4Q onward, driven by rising
adoption rates of new server platform CPU
sockets, which help Lotes reﬂect a higher
cost structure to its end customers.
▪ Adoption of QD and SOCAMM connectors
for AI servers have been rising since 2H26
and should see more contributions into
CY27. NPO socket is also expected to ramp
in CY27 in small volume, with increases in
2028.
▪ We forecast the AI revenue mix will rise

from ~5% in CY5 to slightly more than 20%
by CY28.

and 16.5x 2028e P/E, which we consider
undemanding vs. its current valuation of 17x
2027e P/E.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

營收走勢圖

研究員/張均壕 JUN_HAO1@uni-psg.com

研究部仍看好時碩未來營運，主要是考量液冷散熱零件業務隨著客
戶訂單逐步放量及出貨規模擴大 ，帶動 2H26 業績大幅提升。 此外 ，
航太事業成長前景仍佳，工業事業亦可望持續增長，明年展望依舊
正向。研究部預估時碩 2026 年 EPS 3.52 元(+162%YoY)，2027 年
EPS 4.63 元(+32%YoY)。以明年獲利為評價基礎，給予 20 倍本益
比，目標價 93 元。

重點摘要：

1. 受惠AI趨勢，液冷散熱訂單將帶動3Q26業績強勁增長： 
時碩3Q26合併營收上升至18.18億元(+24%QoQ，+43%YoY)，主
要是受惠AI資料中心液冷相關零組件的貢獻增加，令7、8、9
月單月營收持續創新高 。考量營收擴大， 規模效應顯現， 預期
時碩3Q26毛利率將提升至 24.5%，營益率提升至 8.8%，預估
3Q26稅後淨利1.38億元(+101%QoQ，+34%YoY)，預估3Q26EPS 
1.26元。

2. 液冷散熱產品進入爬坡期，4Q26營運淡季不淡： 
展望4Q26， 雖然年底通常為時碩汽車、工業業務的淡季， 但受
惠散熱零件仍處在出貨爬坡期，預期季度營收與獲利仍有望
再墊高。研究部預估時碩 4Q26合併營收18.85億元(+4%QoQ，
+55%YoY)，4Q26稅後淨利1.40億元(+1%QoQ，+113%YoY)， 預估
4Q26EPS 1.27元 。 全年度來看 ， 研究部預估時碩2026年合併營
收將提升至65.25億元(+31%YoY)，年度毛利率24.1%，營益率
7.1%，稅後淨利3.87億元(+161%YoY)，預估2026年EPS 3.52
元。

3. 航太業務維持成長動能，積極拓展新商機 
受惠於全球航太產業需求穩健及上游關鍵零組件產能吃緊，
時碩管理層持續看好航太業務前景 ，其中天陽航太營運成長
率預估可達15%～20%。 此外 ， 公司積極擴充泰國航太產能 ， 規
劃建置鋁合金及不鏽鋼生產線，以掌握全球航太供應鏈重組
及非紅供應鏈商機 ， 中長期目標為貢獻約10億元營收 。 除既有
航太業務外，時碩亦積極拓展人形機器人及半導體設備等新
興應用領域。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

在人形機器人方面， 時碩已投入諧波減速器及關節零組件開
發，並與國際及中國大陸主要廠商展開合作。半導體設備方
面，時碩持續開發相關精密零組件，未來將配合泰國新廠布
局 ， 爭取台灣客戶海外工廠的供應商機 。 整體而言 ， 隨著航太
業務穩健成長，以及人形機器人與半導體設備等新領域逐步
拓展 ， 將有助於公司建立更多元的營運成長動能 ， 降低對傳統
汽車產業的依賴。

4. 明年業績續升，預估2027年EPS 4.63元： 
展望2027年 ， 考量日系大廠的液冷散熱訂單將延續至明年 ， 而
且還有美系散熱大廠客戶訂單與磁浮壓縮機關鍵訂單等待發
酵 ， 加上航太業務仍具偏強增速 ， 預期時碩2027年業績將持續
提升。研究部預估時碩2027年合併營收74.20億元(+14%YoY)，
年度毛利率 25.0%， 預估2027年EPS將進一步提升至 4.63元。

評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

◼ 結 論 與 建 議 
基 於 以 下 理 由 ， 研究部 維持 力成 買 進 評 等  
(1) 記 憶 體 、 高 階 邏 輯 封 測 需 求 強 勁 ， 營 收 及 獲 利 創 歷 年 單 季 新 高 ， 目 前封
裝 / 測 試 稼 動 率 皆 超 過 90% ， 加 上 產 品 調 價 與 產 品 組 合 改 善 ， 4 Q26 營 收 、
毛 利 率 仍 有 成 長 空 間  
(2) 目 前 稼 動 率 接 近 滿 載 ， DRA M 需 求 持 續 大 於 供 給 ， NA ND 受 惠 企 業 及
SSD 需 求 增 加 ， 邏 輯 業 務 亦 維 持 雙 位 數 成 長 。 且 隨 載 板 等 材 料 成 本 上 升 ，
力 成 依 不 同 產 品 向 客 戶 反 映 成 本 ， 漲 價 幅 度 介 於 個 位 數 至 雙 位 數 ， 有 助 於
維 持 毛 利 率 改 善 趨 勢  
(3) 目 前 包 含 Flip Chip BGA 等 先 進 封 裝 相 關 業 務 已 占 集 團 營 收 30% 以 上 ，
FOPLP 客 戶 及 產 品 種 類 逐 漸 明 確 ， 設 備 取 得 情 況 亦 較 先 前 改 善 。 隨 客 戶 驗
證 完 成 及 量 產 規 模 擴 大 ， 預 期 先 進 封 裝 將 成 為 202 7 年 後 主 要 成 長 動 能  
(4) 與 AMD 、 Broad com 等 大 客 戶 推 進 先 進 封 裝 合 作 ， 布 局 FOPL P 、 CPO
等 技 術 ， 帶 動 2027 年 後 營 收 結 構 轉 型 機 會 。  
預估力成  2026/202 7 年營收為  964.91 /1161.39 億元， YoY 
+28.8% /+20 .4% ， 毛 利 率  22.6% /25.6% ， 營 益 率  16.2% /18.7% ， 稅 後 淨
利  96.39/132.83 億元， YoY +74.1% /+37 .8% ， EPS  12.7/17 .5 元 ， 給予
PE 20 X 評價 ， 目 標 價 35 0 元 。

◼ 重點摘要  
公 司 簡 介  
力成 (6239) 為全球 前 五 大 半 導 體 封 測 廠 ， 在 記 憶 體 領 域 為 全 球 最 大 封 測 廠 ，
記憶體品牌金士頓、東芝為股東之一。 2012 年 入 主 超 豐 (2441) 之 後 ， 切 入
邏輯 IC 封 測 領 域 。 主 要 產 品 為 DRAM 、 Flash 、 以 及 邏 輯 IC 封 測 。 目 前 美
光為力成的最大客戶，其次為東芝 Toshiba 、 金 士 頓 。 主 要 競 爭 對 手 為 ： 日
月 光 集 團 、 京 元 電 、 頎 邦 、 南 茂 等 全 球 前 十 大 台 灣 封 測 廠 。 3Q2 6 服 務 別 營
收比重為 Packaging 67 %、 SiP/Module 9%、 Testin g 2 4%。 產 品 別 營 收 比
重為 DRAM 20 %、 NAND 2 8% 、 SiP/Module 9%、 Log ic 43% 。

90% ， 預 期 4Q26 在 客 戶 需 求 增 加 下 ， 產 能 利 用 率 有 望 進 一 步 提 升 。 在價
格 方面 ， 近期 因 Substra te 等 原 材 料 成 本 上 漲 ， 力 成 已 依 不 同 產 品 及 成 本
變 化 向 客 戶 反 映 價 格 ， 漲 幅 約 介 於 個 位 數 至 雙 位 數 ， 且 客 戶 已 接 受 漲價 。
隨 漲 價 效 益 持 續 反 映 ， 以 及 產 能 逐 步 轉 向 高 附 加 價 值 產 品 ， 有 助 維 持 毛 利
率 改 善 趨 勢 。 高 階 邏 輯 方 面 ，Flip Chip BGA 於 3Q26 導入 7 5 × 7 8 m m大尺
寸 封 裝 ， 4Q26 進 一 步 導 入 7 8 × 8 8 m m規 格 ， 整 合 SoC 與 D RA M 等 元 件 ，
持 續 擴 大 AI ／ HPC 產 品 布 局 。  
2027 年 成 長 動 能 由 既 有 記 憶 體 封 測 延 伸 至 高 階 邏 輯 及 先 進 封 裝 ， 營 收 結 構
逐 步 改 善 。  記 憶 體 方 面 ， 力成 透 過 設 備 汰 換 、 既 有 產 能 優 化 及 高 階 產 品 導
入 提 升 產 出 效 率 。 邏 輯 方 面 ， AI ／ HPC 及 高 階 測 試 需 求 增 加 ， 超 豐 與 Tera 
Probe ／ Ter aPower 持 續 擴 充 相 關 產 能 ， 以 配 合 客 戶 新 產 品 需 求 。 先 進 封
裝 方 面 ， FOPL P 持 續 進 行 客 戶 驗 證 ， 朝 2027 年 量 產 目 標 推 進 ， 設 備 取 得
情況 亦 有 所 改 善 ，2027 ～ 2028 年 擴 產 計 畫 維 持 原 定 進 度 。 隨 新 產 品 陸 續 導
入 ， 以 及 先 進 封 裝 業 務 規 模 擴 大 ， 預 期 可 進 一 步 降 低 集 團 對 傳 統 記 憶 體 封

矽力*-KY (6415 TT) 
資料中心與車載為成長主軸

永豐觀點 
矽力杰 3Q26 營收優於預期，4Q26 展望正向，雖短期毛利率較為波動，不過隨資
料中心與車載訂單加持，以及第四代製程平滲透率提升， 應有助於中長期毛利率回
穩。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年稅後 EPS 11.95 元(原預估 11.59 元)，2027
年稅後 EPS 15.34 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營運拓展，研究部
維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

ESG 評析 
矽力*-KY 企業永續評鑑整體為 B 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面
向平均得分分別為 B+等與 B 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2023 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。

### Page 2

2

矽力杰為電源管理晶片大廠：矽力杰成立於 02/2008，為電源管理 IC 設計公司，
產品線包括 DC/DC 轉換晶片、電池充電管理晶片、過流保護晶片、靜電防護裝置
與 LED 背光驅動晶片、能源監控管理晶片等。矽力杰 2Q26 產品終端應用主要區
分為四大領域，包括消費電子(如平板電腦、STB 與 TV)36%、工業應用(如 LED 照
明、伺服器與安防設備等)35%、資訊產品(如筆電)9%與網路通訊(如路由器、手機
等)6%、 車載14%。國際電源管理晶片設計同業與矽力杰產品類似者為 TI、Maxim、
ON Semi 與 ADI，台系類似的參與廠商則包括致新、茂達等。 
3Q26 營收優於預期 ，4Q26 展望正向 ：矽力杰總計 3Q26 營收 72.83 億元
(+21%QoQ，+53%YoY)，落於研究部原預估 3Q26 營收達成率 102%(研究部原
預估季增 18%)，優於預期，主要係由資料中心為最主要推升力道，再者車載因新
款晶片推出與市占率提升而增 、 消費電子即便市場需求較為疲弱， 但因公司新款晶
片推出，故反而仍能向上。目前展望 4Q26 亦維持正向看法，營收變化主要仍需視
後段封測產能的供給程度而定。研究部預估矽力杰 3Q26 毛利率 47.63%，稅後獲
利 11.32 億元(-28%QoQ，+66%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.9 元，並預估 4Q26 營收 76.9
億元(+5.5%QoQ，+43%YoY)，毛利率 47.8%，稅後獲利 12.33 億元(+9%QoQ，
+53%YoY)，稅後 EPS 3.13 元。 
主要產能瓶頸在後段封測 ：目前矽力杰最主要的產能瓶頸在後段封測的支應，主要
是後段封測供給被 AI 應用排擠，目前除原有封測廠的產能擴充外，公司策略也持
續驗證新封測廠，以其紓解封測產能緊缺問題。至於晶圓產能，則矽力杰因具備第
四代 12 吋製程平台產能(第四代製程平台滲透率由 2025 年的個位數比重 ， 已將提
升至 4Q26 的 20%)，故反而晶圓較能充足的支應，明年第五代製程平台亦將加入
量產範圍。針對 2026 年毛利率走勢仍較有變數，係因影響毛利率因子正負交錯：
正向因子包括公司依據產品與客戶別而有不等的漲價措施 、 第四代製程平台挹注與
產品結構變化，負向因子則包括晶圓/封測成本上升、存貨依據庫齡政策而有損失
或回沖認列等影響。不過研究部認為隨 07/01 已開始進行漲價，理應能消弭部分
製造成本攀升的衝擊，遂預估 2H26 毛利率應能優於 2Q26 水準，中長期隨第四
代製程平台滲透率提升與產品結構變化， 理應有助於毛利率趨勢向上。 相較毛利率
是否獲得顯著提升，目前公司更關心在希冀產能能獲得更充足的支援， 以滿足客戶
的需求。 
資料中心與車載為後續成長主軸：資料中心、車載與工業將成為推升後續成長的主
軸：(1) 資料中心(包括主板/企業級 SSD/光模組)目前營收比重 15%~16%，2027
年 目 前 維 持 預 期>20% 比 重 ， 公 司資 料 中 心 三 大 項 目 比 重 依 序 為光 通 訊
>SSD>MB，其中光通訊與 SSD 個別均為 mid-to-high single digit，MB 比重較
低。公司過往以 400G 以下為主，今年 400G/800G 均已導入，隨市占率逐步提
升，又未來若主板核心電源出貨，更將進一步推動資料中心業務規模擴大。公司亦
表示 PSU 相關產品出貨亦隨伺服器需求而增，公司 PSU 產品包括 AC-DC 
controller/ESD/Boost/ Buck/Temp sensor等，目前針對 VPD 進展還沒那麼快。
(2) 車載營收比重可望由去年的 13%， 攀升至今年的15%~20%， 研究部認為車載
動力來自於公司車載晶片供應品項增加， 每台車的可供應的內含價值擴大， 並且客
戶群攀升，除中國品牌上量外，也進一步拓展海外市場。(3) 儲能與光伏需求，以
及充電樁應用，亦能拓展工業領域的需求。 
維持買進： 研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年營收 258.35 億元(+37%YoY)，毛利率
47.65%，稅後獲利 47.05 億元(+90%YoY)，稅後 EPS 11.95 元(原預估 11.59 元)，
並預估 2027 年營收 341.9 億元(+32%YoY)，毛利率 48.44%，稅後獲利 60.39 億
元(+28%YoY)，稅後 EPS 15.34 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營

營運現況與分析

3Q26 전사 예상 영업이익 100조원 
DS부문 예상 영업이익은 100.0조원(OPM 67.4%, YoY +1538.5%, QoQ +14.5%) 으로, 
달러환율 1,380원기준, 메모리와 비메모리 사업부문 예상 영업손익은 각각 102.0조원,    
-2.0조원으로 추정한다. 1) 메모리반도체 가격 상승세가 이어지는 가운데, HBM4 판매 
본격화에 따른 영향을 고려하면 DRAM/NAND 분기 평균 가격 상승폭은 각각 +18.0%, 
+16.0%으로 추정하고, 2)비메모리반도체 사업 적자는 지속될 전망이기 때문이다. 다만 
특별 성과급 지급에 따른 충당금 영향을 제외하고 선단공정 중심의 파운드리 가동률을 
고려하면 적자폭은 유의미하게 감소한 것으로 파악된다는 점이 긍정적이다.   
SDC부문 예상 영업이익은 1.1조원(OPM 13.0%, YoY -9.6%, QoQ +54.9%)으로, 1)국내 
주력 고객사향 폴더블 스마트폰 판매 호조가 사업부의 외형 성장에 긍정적으로 작용했을 
것으로 추정됨에도 불구하고, 2) 8.6세대 IT OLED 패널 양산 개시에 따른 감가상각비(약 
0.3조원 수준)와 북미 고객 중심의 판가 인하 압박 영향으로 전년동기대비 수익성은 약 
2%pt 훼손이 불가피할 전망이다. DX부문 예상 영업손익은 -1.1조원(YoY 적자전환, QoQ 
적자지속)으로, 메모리반도체를 포함한 IT 부품 가격 부담이 전분기대비 확대되고 있기 
때문이다. MX와 VD/DA사업부 예상 손익은 각각 -0.9조원, -0.2조원으로 추정한다.

투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 63만원으로 상향 조정  
메모리반도체의 가격 상승폭이 다소 둔화되겠지만, 2027~2028년에도 타이트한 수급 
환경이 이어질 전망이라는 점을 주목한다. 1)시장은 신규 증설과 HBM 관련 노이즈로 인한 
2028년 피크아웃 가능성을 우려하고 있으나, HBM 의 범용 DRAM 생산능력 잠식과 AI 
추론 확산에 따른 고용량 메모리 수요를 감안하면 공급 제약의 해소 시점은 예상보다 
늦어질 가능성이 높기 때문이다. 뿐만 아니라, 2)메모리 업사이클이 장기화될 경우 동사 
FCF는 과거대비 구조적으로 확대되어 보다 친화적인 주주환원 정책(2027~2029년, 
2027년 1월 예정)을 제시할 가능성도 배제할 수 없기 때문이다. 동사 2027년 연간 실적 
추정치 상향 조정에 따라 목표주가를 63만원(2027년 BPS * Target PBR 3.5배 적용)으로 
상향 조정한다. 피크아웃 우려에 따른 주가 조정은 중장기 관점에서 매수 기회다.

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82만원에서 64만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

Document 290: Bank bond supply to stay at cruising altitude in 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/Bank bond supply to stay at cruising altitude in 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027.
Redemptions remain the main driver of the high supply, but
the modest macroeconomic outlook is not expected to push
supply up any further. We foresee covered bond issuance
reaching €185bn, senior unsecured bond supply at €210bn and
another €55bn in subordinated bank bonds to be printed

Marine Leleux and Jesse Norcross
High redemption volumes are expected to keep EUR bank bond supply
elevated in 2027
Variables driving next year's bank bond
supply
We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027, down
slightly from the €455bn forecast for 2026. In our view, several
variables will be steering next year’s issuance.
Redemptions will remain the central driver of the elevated 2027
bank bond supply forecast. Nearly €400bn of bonds mature in
2027, up €30bn year-on-year and the highest level in a decade.

### Page 2

The increase is especially signiﬁcant in the covered bond segment
where €186bn will mature, a historic high.
The subordinated segment will also rise, with redemptions totalling
€45bn, up about €6bn from 2026. Of this total, €31bn is in Tier 2
instruments and €14bn in AT1s. The senior unsecured segment will
partially oﬀset these increases, with total redemptions reaching
more than €162bn, down from more than €170bn recorded this
year. The majority stems from senior bail-in bonds (senior non-

preferred or senior HoldCo) with more than €90bn and an
additional €71bn maturing in senior preferred instruments.
Despite diverging trends across segments, elevated redemption
volumes remain the main driver of bank issuance in the euro bond
primary market, as institutions reﬁnance maturing instruments.

While high redemption levels point to stronger supply in 2027,
other factors also play a role, such as the macroeconomic
environment. Eurozone GDP growth is forecast to increase slightly in
the second half of next year to reach 1.5%, still well below levels
seen after the pandemic.
Furthermore, our economists pencil in an additional European
Central Bank rate hike in 2026, bringing policy rates to 2.75% in
early 2027. The higher interest rate environment is not expected to

backdrop acting as a catalyst for bank bond supply in 2027.

Asset swap spread levels initially proved very resilient to

geopolitical instability and related economic changes this year.
Despite some volatility in March, following the outbreak of the US-
Israel conﬂict with Iran, spread levels recovered quickly and reached
levels close to their historic low. However, a broader escalation of
the conﬂict across the Middle East, coupled with the absence of a
clear path to resolution, could put further pressure on spreads.
In addition, budget and political concerns in France and OAT-Bund

volatility, as well as broader European ﬁscal concerns, have recently
led to spreads widening again. The combination of French political
uncertainty and the Middle East conﬂict could trigger some periods
of volatility in the market in 2027. However, we believe these
uncertainties will not be a strong negative for bank bond supply.
Depending on the timing, though, periodic widening may make
ﬁnancial institutions less keen to pre-fund redemptions coming up

in 2028.
Our 2027 supply forecast

### Page 4

When accounting for these three main variables, we expect the EUR
bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027. This translates into a
net supply of €57bn, down from €89bn in 2026.

Our estimate includes €185bn in covered bonds, comprising
€180bn in benchmark-sized issuances, including ﬂoating rate
notes, and €5bn in sub-benchmark bonds. This is a €5bn drop
versus our 2026 forecast. It reﬂects the pre-ﬁnancing already made
by issuers this year in preparation for the historically high
redemptions.
Furthermore, next year’s mortgage lending forecasts point to
stagnation, somewhat limiting any further increase in covered

bond issuance. Besides, with the securitisation package entering its
ﬁnalisation stage, issuers with high funding needs may shift some
secured issuance towards securitisation for diversiﬁcation
purposes. Increasing demand for covered bonds from overseas
investors driven by regulatory developments may also result in
more currency diversiﬁcation, away from EUR-denominated
issuance.
We forecast senior unsecured supply to reach €210bn next year.
This is split between €70bn in senior preferred bonds and €140bn in

senior bail-in instruments. It represents a €10bn YoY decline in

### Page 5

senior preferred supply, reﬂecting both the drop in the segment’s
redemptions from 2027 onwards and the potential impact of the
Crisis Management and Deposit Insurance (CMDI) framework. As the
CMDI reforms are expected to enter into force in 2028, they could
reduce the attractiveness of senior preferred bonds versus senior
bail-in instruments in some EU countries, as senior preferred
exposures will rank lower than, and no longer pari passu to,
uncovered deposits in the creditor hierarchy. The funding cost

advantage of senior preferred unsecured issuance could decline. At
the same time, issuing senior non-preferred bonds could serve the
purpose of increasing the bail-inable layer protecting preferred
senior bonds and their current ratings. This could shift some
issuers’ focus towards the senior non-preferred bucket instead of
the senior preferred segment.
Our forecast for senior non-preferred supply remains stable
compared to our 2026 estimate. While redemptions will increase by

€6bn in 2027 and further in 2028 to reach €102bn, we believe the
impact of higher reﬁnancing needs on non-preferred senior supply
will be partially oﬀset by the lack of balance sheet growth against
the backdrop of higher interest rates. Furthermore, the
outperformance of US dollar debt versus EUR-denominated debt
could see issuers turn more often towards the USD market,
potentially negatively aﬀecting EUR senior bail-in supply.
Lastly, we expect Tier 2 supply to reach €35bn and AT1 supply to

reach €20bn in 2027. This is a €5bn increase YoY in both AT1 and
Tier 2 compared to our 2026 forecast, mainly driven by higher
redemption levels. With AT1 redemptions in 2027 increasing by
€3bn YoY to €14bn, net supply is likely to be marginally higher next
year. In Tier 2, redemptions increase by €4bn to €31bn, essentially
meaning that net supply will only be marginally higher.
As the chart below highlights, net supply in AT1 has been
signiﬁcant in recent years but has slowed this year as the focus has

shifted towards reﬁnancing. As such, while we expect more AT1
supply next year compared to 2026, the levels are relatively in line
with the longer-term trend.

The main driver of supply will also be reﬁnancing for subordinated
capital. We expect a further marginal decline in CET1 buﬀers,
although most banks are already operating at target AT1 and Tier 2
ratios. Still, there are some issuers with substantial gaps in either
AT1 or Tier 2 ratios, which they may look to improve, leading to a

slight increase in supply.
Signiﬁcant Risk Transfer (SRT) issuance has also increased in recent
years and will continue to grow as a tool for banks in terms of
balance sheet and capital management, while it is also growing as
an investable asset class. Although SRTs provide capital and RWA
relief, they do not replace the gone-concern and loss-absorption
capacity of AT1 and Tier 2 instruments. As such, they play a more
complementary role in capital management, and we do not expect

it to have a major impact on bank capital issuance in 2027.
Bank M&A could also be a wildcard for 2027 issuance. With several
large M&A transactions potentially taking place, this may lead to
some capital optimisation and less incremental supply needs;
conversely, goodwill eﬀects, potential regulatory increases in buﬀer
requirements or RWA inﬂation may lead to a slight increase in the
need for capital. However, on the whole, we would expect M&A to

have a slight dampening eﬀect on supply.

Document 311: Cliff Notes_ managing expectations (2026-10-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ managing expectations (2026-10-29).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:04 October 23 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’ attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor
Hauser's fireside chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a follow-up rate hike at the
November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook was "materially worse" than the RBA's

### Page 2

August forecasts. Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having "crystallised" ahead of the
September meeting – the Middle East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser noted the
Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in
August”. He then positioned AI and data centre developments among an "extraordinary sequence of
positive demand shocks around the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through into

CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy Governor noted that "inflation's been coming
in at or a little bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the labour market". He went on to
state that the staff are currently assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement on
capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other words, revise up their assumption for the
NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of market pricing for a follow-up November

hike, from just a 20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition today ahead of next week's
Q3 CPI. For that release, we are forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a 0.9 (3.5%yr)
increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3
as well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast. Provided inflation prints broadly in line
with our expectations, the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.

Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3 and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to
3.7%yr. Under the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely watched measures of core
inflation took a step higher to now be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our NZ
economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October
policy review, while retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.

Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised to the upside in Q3, GDP growth accelerating from
0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on track to
achieve their 4.5-5.0% full-year growth objective.
While annual retail sales growth improved in September, rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace
of growth is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very weak versus history. Consumer
spending continues to be constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to employment

prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five years into the property downturn, house prices continue
to decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep downturn, -20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in
high-tech investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst domestically oriented businesses
as well as property’s malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and 2028 as the

contribution from net exports moderates, broad-based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect
recent targeted support measures to be broadened and given much greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr in September, exceeding both market
expectations and the Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely driven by energy, fuel
prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services

inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an immediate concern inflation, the labour market
showing resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to 4.7% in August, suggests demand

### Page 3

Related articles
risks for inflation will need close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is
released. For both events, the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The week after, the
US mid-term elections are due.

 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -

to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is

 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s

supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022

Document 334: Eurozone _ Growth on track despite the turbulence (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone _ Growth on track despite the turbulence (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

8
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
GROWTH ON TRACK DESPITE THE TURBULENCE
Growth was stronger than expected in Q2 and is set to strengthen further in the coming quarters. According to our fore-
casts, it will reach 1.5% in 2027 after 1.1% in 2026. This growth is expected to be driven by investment and its knock-on
effect on exports. On an annual average basis, inflation is expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and 2.8% in 2027, peaking at

the end of 2026. The ECB is expected to continue its monetary tightening. At this stage, a single further rate rise, aimed at
keeping inflation expectations anchored, is expected in December. The impact of rising interest rates (key policy rates and
market rates) on public finances and private sector borrowing conditions will be worth monitoring.
GROWTH: BEYOND THE SHOCKS, A RESILIENT MOMENTUM
Growth in the Eurozone remained robust in the first half of 2026, at

0.25% q/q in Q1 and 0.33% q/q in Q2, excluding Ireland (where economic
activity is volatile). It was driven by Germany, where signs of a recovery
are becoming clearer (see Germany). Our baseline scenario forecasts
growth of 1.1% in 2026, followed by 1.5% in 2027 on an annual average
basis. This growth is expected to be driven by the strengthening of
trends already underway in public and private investment (particularly
in defence and AI) and in exports (trade in services and intra-zone trade

in goods). Household consumption is likely to be resilient too. According
to our forecasts, job creation would continue to underpin household
incomes, thereby cushioning the impact of inflation.
A NEW LEASE OF LIFE FOR INDUSTRY
The Eurozone is undergoing one of the most profound industrial trans-
formations in its history. This is giving rise to divergences between key
sectors but, above all, to new drivers of growth whose effects are beco-
ming increasingly apparent (see our analysis). Indeed, the signs of an

economic recovery are growing stronger. Business sentiment indicators
show a marked improvement in the economic outlook across most sec-
tors (Chart 2), driven primarily by electronics (AI, digitalisation), the
automotive sector and aerospace. European industry is thus benefi -
ting from strong demand for capital goods; a structural trend that will
continue to be fuelled by ongoing investment cycles in AI, defence and
electrification. The introduction of minimum European content quotas

in the draft law on the industrial accelerator, together with the Com -
mission’s intention to tighten the criteria for access to European public
procurement markets, will provide additional support for European in-
dustry in the longer term.
FOREIGN TRADE: A REPOSITIONING THAT IS PAYING OFF
Following a decline in 2026, linked to rising energy import costs, the
trade surplus is expected to rise to around 3.0% of GDP in 2027 (of
which 2% of GDP for goods and 1% for services). Growth in European ex-

ports has slowed compared with its pre-Covid trend and is being ham-
pered by the decline in Chinese and US markets. However, Eurozone
exports in volume rose significantly in Q2 (+3.4% q/q).
More importantly, since Liberation Day (April 2025), Eurozone coun -
tries have found significant sources of growth amongst other European
partners, which has enabled them to cushion the decline in exports to
certain third markets. This is evident in exports to the rest of the EU,

EFTA countries (Switzerland in particular) and, to a lesser extent, the
United Kingdom, Ukraine and the Balkans (Chart 3). The momentum of
intra-European trade therefore remains very strong. It is expected to
strengthen further with the anticipated investments in defence, AI and
electrification.
FINANCING: FINANCIAL CONDITIONS ARE EXPECTED TO REMAIN TIGHT
Financial conditions in the Eurozone remained tight in Q2 2026, accor-
ding to indices calculated by the ECB (including the BIG [Broad Inter -

mediation Gauge]). They are expected to tighten more significantly in
the second half of the year: i/ the rise in long-term bond yields, which
has accelerated since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran, is expected to
continue; and ii/ the ECB will continue its monetary tightening (+25 bps

9
EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
in Q3, then in Q4). For the time being, the debt burden is still weighing
only moderately on public finances due to the long average maturity of
countries’ debt (8.2 years on average in the Eurozone in July), but this
burden will increase by 2030.
On the corporate front, the rise in interest rates has remained contained
(+2 bps since January 2026, reaching 3.9% in July), which has contri -

buted to the strong growth in new investment loans (cumulated over
one year, +9.1% y/y in July compared with 15.3% y/y in September 2025).
By contrast, cumulative net debt securities issues over one year re -
mained stable in July, whilst cumulative net issues of listed shares over
one year halved month on month.
Rising interest rates will continue to hamper new variable-rate
home loans to households (around 65% of new lending) more signi -
ficantly than fixed-rate loans, for which rates stood in July at 3.69%

(+4 bps m/m) and 3.36% (+2 bps m/m) respectively. New home loans,
which are stable in 2026 on a cumulative over one year basis, are there-
fore unlikely to see any improvement. A fall in real-estate transactions
could weigh on new consumer credit through a reduction in expenditure
on home furnishings and renovations, whereas consumer credit has
been fairly buoyant so far (year-to-date, +5.2% y/y in July compared
with +1.4% y/y in January).
LABOUR MARKET: RESILIENCE YET TO BE CONFIRMED

Employment in the Eurozone rose again by an average of 0.1% q/q in
the first half of 2026, driven by Spain and Portugal. Given the growth
outlook in these two countries, this momentum is likely to continue,
whilst business sentiment surveys in Germany suggest that employ -
ment is beginning to stabilise. Nevertheless, employment levels are
plateauing (France, the Netherlands) or even falling in other countries
(Austria, Finland), which is likely to slow the decline in the Eurozone

unemployment rate, meaning it is expected to remain close to current
levels in 2027. Furthermore, second-round effects of the rise in inflation
on wages cannot be ruled out: the ECB’s wage tracker suggests wage
growth of close to 3% by summer 2027 (2.9% y/y in June 2027, compared
with 2.6% in September).
INFLATION: A PEAK EXPECTED IN LATE 2026
Changes in wage dynamics will be a key factor in the inflation out -
look for 2027, whilst energy price rises are picking up again, driven

by the conflict in the Middle East. This rise is even sharper than in
the spring for gas (linked to restocking ahead of winter) and is likely
to feed through to electricity prices in several countries. We therefore
anticipate inflation peaking at 3.7% y/y in Q4 2026, before easing as the
energy component falls. On an annual average, headline inflation is
expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and then at 2.8% in 2027.
At this stage, the pass-through of rising energy prices to other com -

ponents of the consumer price index, particularly services, remains li -
mited. The alternative core indicators monitored by the ECB, including
the PCCI (Persistent and Common Component of Inflation, 2.1% in July),
remain close to the 2% target. Core inflation, however, is expected to
peak two months later than headline inflation (3% in March 2027).
Inflationary risks are nevertheless tilted to the upside. These are lin -
ked to food prices, which are expected to be affected by the energy

shock and weather conditions (summer drought, El Niño); they could
also stem from a delayed impact of energy price rises on underlying
inflation. The rise in prices of energy-dependent inputs (notably che -
micals and plastics) and those linked to the AI sector is expected to be
sustained. Growth, which we expect to remain robust over the coming
quarters, will also fuel underlying inflationary pressures.
MONETARY POLICY: TIGHTENING WITHOUT SLOWING (GROWTH)

Following two 25-basis-point rises (in June and September), we ex -
pect a third in December, which would bring the deposit facility rate
to 2.75%. It would then be in slightly restrictive territory (we estimate
the upper bound of the neutral rate to be 2.5%). This rise would be
aimed primarily at anchoring inflation expectations and, consequently,
at curbing the pass-through effects of inflation. Monetary tightening
would therefore remain limited. Indeed, the overshoot of the inflation

target would be temporary, and the operational framework adopted by
the ECB in June 2025 allows it to tolerate this, provided it remains brief.
However, it cannot be ruled out that the ECB might take further action
if the decline in inflation proves slower than expected.
PUBLIC FINANCES ARE CONTINGENT ON RISING INTEREST RATES
The fiscal impulse in the Eurozone is expected to be clearly positive in
2026 due to the rise in defence and infrastructure spending in Germany.

This will contribute to a widening of the country’s primary deficit (from
+0.9 pp of GDP to 2.8%, according to our forecasts). The improvement in
public finances is expected to stall in both France and Italy, at least in
2026, due to the impact of inflation on the debt burden via index-linked
bonds. This trend is expected to reverse in 2027 with less expansionary
fiscal policies. Nevertheless, the increase in the debt service burden
would fuel a further rise in the public deficit, which would thus reach

around 3.3% and 3.5% of GDP in 2026 and 2027 respectively (compared
with 2.9% in 2025). Against this backdrop, the Eurozone’s public debt-
to-GDP ratio is projected to exceed 90% of GDP in 2026 and reach 93%
of GDP in 2027, according to our forecasts.
Article completed on 15 September 2026

Global air travel growth is set to rebound to 5% in 2027, but
airlines face tougher conditions. Demand remains resilient, but
the fuel price shock will spill over into 2027, while new aircraft
supply remains constrained. This creates divergent fortunes
across airlines, segments and regions
Rico Luman
The aviation industry now faces a growing challenge to deliver proﬁtable
growth as capacity remains constrained, costs rise, and geopolitical risks
intensify

could account for close to 35% of airlines’ operating
costs in 2026, up from 27% in 2025. Tight reﬁnery
capacity and low inventories are likely to keep prices
elevated even if oil prices ease.

initially protected many European airlines, but that cover
will progressively expire during 2027. US fares have

Jet fuel continues to trade high in Q4 2026
European jet fuel prices in $ per tonne (ARA)
Source: Reﬁnitiv, ING Research
Cost shock hit proﬁtability, but for some
airlines more than others

Jet fuel is often the largest or second-largest cost item for airlines.
In 2025, it accounted for 27% of total operational costs on average.
This share could move towards 35% in 2026 as a result of
persistently high jet fuel prices and elevated crack spreads, caused
by the loss of Middle Eastern supplies and tight global availability.
Further geopolitical developments remain highly uncertain. Even if
the Middle East conﬂict is resolved and oil prices retreat, tight

reﬁnery capacity and depleted inventories are likely to keep middle-
distillate prices, including jet fuel, elevated well into 2027.
High fuel prices impact proﬁts in US and
Europe, low cost carriers most vulnerable

Operational proﬁt (EBIT) in % per peiod

Fuel hedging provides less cushioning in
2027; fares must respond
Airlines employ very diﬀerent strategies to protect themselves
against fuel price shocks, using derivatives based on crude oil or jet
fuel. European airlines generally hedged 40-90% of their jet fuel
consumption, cushioning the initial impact of the price shock.
However, the Middle East conﬂict has lasted too long to avoid a

signiﬁcant impact. Existing hedges gradually roll oﬀ, increasing
exposure to prevailing market prices. Ryanair, which remains (80%)
hedged until the second quarter of 2027, has already warned of
signiﬁcantly higher fares.
Outside, the picture is mixed, but hedge ratios are generally lower,
and airlines in the US and China, for instance, don’t hedge at all.
This means that two airlines operating similar routes can face very
diﬀerent fuel bills.
Airline tickets in the US responded much

faster to the cost shock than in Europe

Fares already sharply higher in the US, less
so in Europe
The fuel price shock will increasingly feed through into higher fares.
US airlines’ practice of retaining relatively high exposure to fuel price
volatility resulted in an almost immediate pass-through, with ticket
prices rising solidly in the months after jet fuel prices started to rise.

It is also easier to raise prices when domestic competitors face a
similar cost shock.
The situation is diﬀerent in Europe, where hedging provided more
initial protection. This also means fares may still need to rise
signiﬁcantly if fuel prices fail to fall. Coverage gradually expires as
2027 progresses, leaving airlines increasingly exposed to higher
spot prices.

proﬁtable

### Page 9

Source: IATA, *ING Research based on IATA
More factors are driving up costs,
particularly in Europe
Jet fuel is the clear outlier this year, but wage and maintenance
costs are also rising faster than the average price level. Borrowing
costs and aircraft leasing rates remain another factor, although the
impact depends on individual airlines’ strategies and ﬁnancial
structures.
European airlines also face rising costs from the European Union
Emissions Trading System (ETS) for ﬂights within the European

Economic Area. In addition, fuel suppliers are required to blend
sustainable aviation fuel (SAF) under ReFuelEU Aviation, starting at
2% and rising to 6% by 2030*. SAF still trades at a signiﬁcant
premium. This puts a price on emissions, and in combination, this
adds some $300 per tonne of jet fuel in 2026 (BNEF, ING Research).
Meanwhile, France and the Netherlands have either raised or plan
to raise ticket taxes, particularly on long-haul ﬂying, while Germany

has scaled its levy back and Sweden has abolished it.
High fuel prices put more pressure on low-
cost carriers

then be in scope. **In 2025, the blend rate stood at 2.8%

### Page 10

Low-cost carriers are particularly exposed because fuel accounts
for a larger share of their cost base, and proﬁt bases are more
vulnerable. High hedge ratios initially cushion volatility, but these
hedges gradually roll oﬀ. The more challenging environment is
already visible in the performance of several European low-cost
carriers and will remain an important theme in 2027 if fuel prices
stay elevated.
Premium carriers have more room to manoeuvre. Demand for

premium seats (business, premium economy) is less price-
sensitive, providing airlines with greater scope to pass on higher
costs. Both United Airlines and Delta Airlines reported little impact
after price increases of 15-20% in the second to third quarters.
Passengers are booking later, but airlines continue to indicate a
strong propensity to ﬂy, especially in premium and premium-
economy classes. A minority also accounts for a large share of
ﬂights. In the Netherlands, for example, 25% of people account for

75% of journeys. These frequent travellers tend to be less price-
sensitive.
There has therefore been some demand destruction wiping out part
of the underlying growth. So far, though, the propensity to travel
has remained resilient.
Aviation remains proﬁtable, but margins hit
a post-pandemic low
The fuel shock has signiﬁcantly aﬀected margins. On average,
proﬁts in the global industry are expected to drop meaningfully
below 5%. Airlines including Ryanair, Southwest Airlines and AirAsia

have scaled back capacity on unproﬁtable routes as elevated fuel
prices persisted longer than expected.
According to global aviation consultancy Cirium, around 1% of
global air travel capacity will be removed heading into the Northern
Hemisphere winter of 2026-27. In less mature markets, maintaining
volumes while passing on the higher fuel bill is more diﬃcult.
Airlines may also prioritise less fuel-eﬃcient, older-generation
aircraft over capacity growth.

### Page 11

Industry headwinds could become a catalyst for further
consolidation, ranging from minority stakes to full takeovers.
Fares could retreat if fuel prices ease, but
don't expect too much
Dynamic and algorithm-based pricing is becoming increasingly
common. This helps airlines optimise already historically high load
factors (84% in 2026 so far) but can also contribute to greater fare
volatility, especially as passengers book later amid uncertainty.
If fuel prices ease in 2027, fares could drop back. Yet relatively

strong demand, constrained aircraft availability and general
inﬂation mean a return to early-2026 fare levels looks unlikely.
Cargo beneﬁts from high-tech demand and
supply-chain volatility
Air cargo continues to perform well as a fast transport option in an
increasingly volatile and less predictable world. Volume growth
remained almost 5% year-to-date, while freight rates remained
attractive for carriers.
While e-commerce drove much of the growth in recent years,

digitalisation and large-scale AI investment increasingly support
shipments of high-value technology products. Strong cargo
performance helped cushion pressure on airline results in 2026.
European carriers also beneﬁted from reduced available freight
capacity from Middle Eastern competitors.
Freighter capacity received a boost during the pandemic through
passenger-to-freighter conversions, but that boost has largely run
its course. New-generation A350 and Boeing 777 freighters should

add capacity in the coming years.
Aircargo volume resilient in disruptive
geopolitical environment

### Page 12

Source: IATA, ING Research
Future demand 2027 and onwards
Aviation’s centre of gravity is increasingly
shifting eastwards
The centre of gravity of global aviation is increasingly shifting
eastwards. Trips per capita are expected to see strong increases
over the next two decades, particularly in Asia. This matters even
more because these markets are far more populous.
Until recently, around 90% of the world’s population did not ﬂy in a
given year, but this is gradually changing. As a result, ﬂeet growth is

expected to be strongest in Asia, with its share of the global aircraft
ﬂeet increasing from around 33% to approximately 45%.
People still expected to ﬂy more, especially
in India

### Page 13

Source: S&P, Airbus, ING Research
India is becoming a powerhouse for future
airline growth
Air travel is strongly correlated with GDP and household income. As
Asia’s middle class expands, the potential customer base for air
travel swells. For future aviation demand, this income eﬀect is even
more important than population growth alone.
Younger generations also appear keen to travel, adding to the
potential in countries such as India and Indonesia. Increasingly

internationally dispersed families provide another structural source
of demand.
India stands out here. Strong economic growth, rising household
incomes and a huge population oﬀer strong potential. Airlines are
positioning themselves accordingly, illustrated by historically large
aircraft orders from IndiGo and Air India. India’s aircraft ﬂeet is
expected to grow by around 8% annually on average over the next
two decades.
China and India are the growth engines of

global aviation in the decades ahead

### Page 14

Source: S&P Global, Airbus, ING Research
Supply-side developments
Aircraft scarcity keeps capacity tight and
lease rates elevated
Supply constraints have been curbing aviation growth since the
post-pandemic recovery and remain a major factor. The share of
stored aircraft dropped below 10% by the end of 2025, its lowest
level since 2019. Even several A380s (the largest aircraft) have
returned to service.
More than half of the global commercial aircraft ﬂeet is leased.

Tight capacity is consequently reﬂected in relatively high used-
aircraft values and lease rates.
Airbus and Boeing had a combined record backlog of around
15,000 aircraft by mid-2026. Production and deliveries continue to
lag order intake after seven consecutive years of subdued deliveries
from 2019 onwards. Demand for new-generation aircraft remains
strong, as illustrated by major recent orders from Avolon and
Turkish Airlines.
Production could ﬁnally return to pre-pandemic levels in 2027.

Chinese manufacturer COMAC, meanwhile, is making progress in
China in Asia, but Western certiﬁcation remains distant.

### Page 15

New airplane deliveries continue on a
recovery track now with Boeing rebounding
Number of delivered commercial airplanes by manufacturers Airbus &
Boeing per year
Source: Company reports *Company forecast
Why aircraft supply remains constrained
Production has still not caught up with pre-pandemic levels
and is unlikely to do so in the next ﬁve years. Aircraft
manufacturing is recovering, but delays and large backlogs
push delivery dates into the 2030s. Airlines are therefore forced

to keep older aircraft ﬂying for longer.
Suppliers are still struggling to ramp up. Six years after the
sharp pandemic downturn, aviation supply chains have still
not fully normalised. Stringent safety and certiﬁcation
requirements mean manufacturers rely on a limited number of
specialised suppliers. Engines and sophisticated cabin
components still have long lead times, while skilled workers
remain scarce.
Engine issues continue to aﬀect available capacity. The peak

impact of the Pratt & Whitney inspection programme after an
identiﬁed production issue has passed, but checks and
maintenance continue to constrain parts of the A320neo-
family ﬂeet and absorb scarce maintenance capacity.

Aircraft production still has a long way to go
catching up: backlog hit fresh record in

Orderbook Airbus & Boeing: number of commercial airplanes
Source: Company reports
Delivery catch-up is crucial for both
capacity and efﬁciency
New aircraft are critical not only to accommodate growth, but also
to improve eﬃciency. The average age of the global ﬂeet has
increased from around 13 to 15 years since 2019 due to production
constraints.
This matters increasingly as older units require more maintenance

at a time when skilled personnel and maintenance capacity are
scarce; they also consume signiﬁcantly more fuel and therefore
emit more.
Boeing is ﬁnally regaining altitude
supporting deliveries
Boeing is ﬁnally making a sustained comeback following an
unprecedented period of disruption stemming from the 737 MAX
accidents, the pandemic and subsequent production and quality
problems. It received certiﬁcation for its 737 MAX 7 and also the
737 MAX 10 recently. Boeing plans to increase production for the

family from 38 to 47 a month.

### Page 17

Higher 737 production should support deliveries in 2027 and
gradually bring pre-pandemic production levels back into sight. This
is hugely important for Boeing-dependent airlines such as Ryanair
and Southwest. Progress on certiﬁcation of the 777-9 is also
important for long-haul ﬂeet renewal.
Fleet renewal is currently the most
important decarbonisation lever
Air travel continues to grow, and associated emissions tend to
continue to rise further as well. Sustainable aviation fuels are

labelled as an important industry pathway to decarbonise, but
globally, blend rates still hover below 1% in 2026.
The closure of airspaces, too, has created ineﬃciencies and extra
emissions over the last few years, undermining gains elsewhere.
Around 63% of the global ﬂeet still consists of previous-generation
aircraft. Replacing them can reduce fuel consumption by 10-25% or
more, making ﬂeet renewal one of the most eﬀective near-term
decarbonisation levers available to airlines.

The challenge is to deliver proﬁtable growth
in a more disruptive world
For airlines, the challenge heading into 2027 is therefore not simply

ﬁnding suﬃcient demand. Relatively poor punctuality, with only
around 61% of ﬂights departing on time in 2026 year-to-date, is a
signal.
The globally operating industry faces more turbulence and
uncertainty amid intensiﬁed geopolitical risks. The challenge is to
deliver proﬁtable growth in a world of persistently constrained
capacity, higher costs and more frequent disruption.

### Page 1

苹果推出搭载 M6 芯⽚的 24 英⼨ iMac （起售价 12499 元）与 14 英⼨⼊⻔级 MacBook Pro
（起售价 15999 元），将于 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇正式发货。 M6 芯⽚带来 AI 性能五倍提升，图形处理速度
提升 30% 。此外，苹果本周三将发布⾸款触控屏 MacBook Pro ，据报道预计成为史上最贵
Mac 笔记本， 14 英⼨售价约 2899 美元， 16 英⼨⾄少 3399 美元。
摘要：
苹果公司本周密集发布新品，在触控屏 MacBook Pro 亮相前⼣，率先推出搭载 M6 芯⽚的 24 英⼨
iMac 与 14 英⼨⼊⻔级 MacBook Pro 。
两款新机将于 10 ⽉ 29 ⽇凌晨 1 点 10 分接受预购， 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇正式发货。新款 iMac 起售价 12499 元⼈
⺠币， MacBook Pro 起售价 15999 元。
苹果表⽰，M6 芯⽚赋予新款 iMac 在⼈⼯智能任务上较上代机型五倍的性能提升，⼊⻔级
MacBook Pro 的图形处理速度则较前代提升 30% 。

此次发布是苹果本周新品攻势的第⼆波，周⼀该公司刚更新了 iPad mini ，新增屏幕升级与防⽔
功能。华尔街⻅闻提及，苹果将于周三以其史上⾸款触控屏 MacBook Pro 为本周产品发布收
尾。
据彭博社报道，该款触屏 Mac 将成为该公司最贵的笔记本，已经探讨将新款 14 英⼨ Mac 售价定
在 2899 美元， 16 英⼨机型⾄少 3399 美元。
M6 芯⽚成核⼼升级，外观延续前代设计
新款 iMac 与 MacBook Pro 在外观上与前代产品⾼度相似，主要变化集中于内部硬件。两款机型
均搭载 M6 芯⽚ —— 该芯⽚于今年 9 ⽉随 Mac mini ⾸次亮相。

的⼊⻔级 MacBook Pro ； M7 Ultra 芯⽚与新款 Mac Studio 则被安排在 2028 年推出。
苹果正持续推进电脑产品线的全⾯⾰新，以满⾜⽇益增⻓的⼈⼯智能应⽤需求，此次发布的两

Document 393: NZ labour market preview, Q3 2026 (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ labour market preview, Q3 2026 (2026-10-28).pdf

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Turning point?
• We expect the unemployment rate to edge
down to 5.5% for the September quarter.
• Job numbers have been gaining momentum
in recent months, and now look to be enough
to outstrip population growth. However, the
participation rate will be the deciding factor.
• Despite cost-of-living pressures on workers,
the existing slack in the labour market is likely
to keep wage growth subdued.
• Our forecasts are a little more positive than

what the RBNZ assumed in their September
policy statement. Assuming they deliver on
an OCR hike this afternoon, the labour market
surveys could have an important bearing on
whether they follow up with another hike in
December.

The New Zealand economy has returned to growth over
the last year, despite a number of headwinds. That’s been
accompanied by some modest growth in jobs, though
to date it hasn’t been enough to prevent a further rise

in the unemployment rate. However, we think that next
Wednesday’s labour market reports could show the jobs
market reaching a turning point.
We expect the unemployment rate to ease from 5.6%
to 5.5% in the September quarter, which would mark
the first fall in almost five years. That’s not a firmly held
view, given the usual margin of uncertainty around this
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Preview of Q3 labour market surveys:
4 November, 10:45am.
28 Oct 2026 | Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
measure. But we’re now seeing consistent evidence
that jobs growth is outstripping the modest pace of
population growth. We also note that the previous result
was 5.56% in unrounded terms, so it wouldn’t take much
to tip the rounded rate down to 5.5% this time.
On the whole, our forecasts are a little stronger than
what the RBNZ assumed in its September Monetary
Policy Statement. While our unemployment rate forecast

is identical, we’re more positive on both employment
and labour force participation. Meanwhile, our pick of a
0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index is slightly ahead of the
RBNZ’s estimate of 0.4%.
Assuming that the RBNZ delivers the OCR hike that we
expect this afternoon, we think they will be noncommittal
about the chances of another hike at the 9 December
Monetary Policy Statement. In that respect, the labour
market surveys could be important in setting market

expectations for the December review, as there aren’t
that many major data releases between now and then.
Forecast details.
We expect the Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS)
to show a 0.5% rise in employment for the September
quarter. That’s broadly in line with the Monthly
Employment Indicator (MEI), with the September release
this morning showing a third straight month of growth
in filled jobs. We note that employment growth in the
HLFS has actually outstripped the MEI for the last nine

quarters, but it’s not clear why this has been the case and
we’re wary of extrapolating it too far into the next quarter.

Employment growth of around 0.5% would be more than
enough to absorb the modest 0.3% rise in the working-
age population, and on its own would suggest a fall in

the unemployment rate. But it also comes down to the
rate of labour force participation, which we don’t really
have much guidance on. The participation rate has been
choppy over recent quarters, and in the June quarter
we saw an unusually large 0.3pt rise to 70.7%; we’re
assuming some payback this time with a more modest
0.1pt rise to 70.8%.
We’ll also be keeping an eye on the broader indicators of
slack in the labour market, although we don’t formally use

these in our forecasts. The June quarter saw a sharp rise
in the underutilisation rate from 12.9% to 13.8%. However,
we think there’s some residual seasonality in this series,
relating to one component in particular: people who are
available for work but not actively looking. In that case,
we could see a substantial pullback in the underutilisation
measure this time.
Previously we’ve characterised the labour market as a
“low-fire, low-hire” environment, with much of the rise in

unemployment reflecting the difficulty of breaking into
the workforce, rather than job losses. There are some
signs of a thaw emerging, with job vacancies picking up
from their lows over the last year and business surveys
pointing to a lift in hiring intentions. But we still expect
that it will be a long road to recovery – our current
forecasts see the unemployment rate at 5% by the end of
next year.
While cost-of-living pressures are again rearing their

head as a major concern for workers, the existing degree
of slack in the labour market will continue to make it
difficult for them to push for bigger pay increases. We
expect a 0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index (LCI) for
the September quarter, which would nudge the annual
growth rate up slightly to 2.1%. That said, we don’t have a
lot to go on for this forecast. The MEI includes a measure
of median weekly earnings, which has shown no sign of
acceleration so far – but this is only a very loose indicator

of wage pressures, as it will also reflect any changes in
the composition of jobs and pay rates.

Document 395: October ECB cheat sheet_ French malaise shouldn’t prompt a dovish turn

Source PDF: all report pdf/October ECB cheat sheet_ French malaise shouldn’t prompt a dovish turn.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Markets will scan the European Central Bank announcement on
29 October for any signs that French bond turmoil has
triggered a dovish rethink. We don’t think it has, and expect
the ECB to leave the door open for a December hike, which
should shield rates and the euro from a major adjustment. As
usual, we also look at three alternative scenarios to our
baseline

Francesco Pesole, Michiel Tukker and Carsten Brzeski
The ECB is expected to leave rates on hold this week at 2.5%
This is our market preview of October's European Central
Bank meeting; you can ﬁnd our macro preview here.
In September, the ECB eﬀectively brushed aside rising bond market
concerns with a hawkish rate hike. This week, a hold is widely
expected. The key question is whether the latest turmoil in French

### Page 2

and other European bonds is enough to trigger a dovish shift in the
ECB’s message.
We don’t think we are there yet. But equally, we admit that a more
dovish tone on further tightening would probably be the ﬁrst line of
defence against more bond volatility, before exploring any bond-
buying measure.
Four scenarios for October
As usual, we look at four scenarios for this meeting. As in previous
instances, the market reaction will depend as much on the

statement and press conference as on the now-customary tip to
the media on Thursday afternoon. In September, the message was:
“ECB oﬃcials expect more tightening, with October in play”.
A hike this week would, however, be a big surprise (close to zero
chance priced in), as inﬂation has remained well-behaved, France
has prompted a dovish repricing and President Christine Lagarde
has sounded a bit less hawkish.
In a meeting without new projections, the wording around

December will be crucial. We expect the door to remain open for a
December move, with a broadly neutral and balanced message to
markets. Signalling that a hike by year-end is “likely” would be a
slightly hawkish surprise in our view, even if markets are pricing in
20bp.

### Page 3

Rates look for predictability amid fragile
sentiment
Rates have started to deviate from their tight correlation with oil,
but global factors remain an important driver. Risk sentiment is
more fragile, and not just because of the French turmoil, but also
because of renewed AI jitters. So whilst markets will listen carefully
to Lagarde, we don’t expect this week’s meeting to trigger a major
repricing. A stronger dedication to a December hike would nudge a

hawkish repricing, but with more than 20bp already priced in, the
reaction should be contained. Both the ECB and markets
understand the outlook is shrouded in uncertainty, so any forward
guidance will be weighed against that backdrop.
A bigger surprise to markets would be a 25bp hike, but this would
not be taken lightly by investors, especially if December remains in
play too. Without clear second-round inﬂation risks, an unexpected
tightening of ﬁnancial conditions while sentiment is fragile could

trigger a broader risk-oﬀ move. Expect French government bond
spreads to widen and equities to continue their drift lower. We
might even see 10yr Bund yields fall on the decision, as growth
concerns return.
EUR could do with some ECB protection
The euro has become less sensitive to the latest moves in French
bond spreads, with undervaluation to short-term fair value still
around 1-1.5%. Still, the common currency has been hurt not only
by the build-up of a ﬁscal risk premium, but also by the repricing

lower in ECB rate expectations because of the French turmoil.
This week’s meeting is unlikely to have the same FX impact as
September’s, but currency investors will be looking for any cracks in
the ECB’s tightening bias as a sign that bond market concerns
could prompt a dovish rethink.
That probably leaves the balance of risks on the downside for the
euro. Still, our baseline is for no pushback against lingering hawkish
bets, leaving the euro broadly stable after Thursday.
